### WEL
  
abbreviation. Wool Equities Ltd, the successor to the Wool Board  
> Wool Equities Ltd (WEL) chief executive Mark O'Grady is expecting substantial returns from operating assets which are not being given any value on the sharemarket. WEL, the company spun out of the old Wool Board, is trading on the NZAX market at 50c a share... *(Sep 29, 2004,NZ Farmers' Weekly,20)*

### welcome swallow
  
noun. a self-introduced forked-tailed bird, Hirundo tahitica, with chestnut face and throat.(an Australian immigrant)  
> In less than 50 years, welcome swallows have established themselves widely in New Zealand, particularly near water but also nesting under verandahs in town. *(May 2002,Forest & Bird,26)*  
> Wander down to the seaward face to get the best look at the strange sandstone formations; tucked under some of the overhangs are the daubed mud nests of welcome swallows. *(Dec 30, 2003,Dominion Post,C2)*  
> Swallows live life at breakneck speed that forces the rest of nature into a spectator role as they perform their incredible aerobatics. *(Jul 16, 2005,Dominion Post,E18)*  
> **Moon** Welcome swallows inhabit open country, swamps, rivers and the coast. *(2002,Birds of New Zealand,109)*

### welfare
  
noun. in NZ (as in Britain) used generally of social welfare work, and concretely of the (former) Child Welfare Division [?Branch] of the Department of Education [check] or of Social Security [?now Department of Social Welfare]: and used contemptuously in collocations or as an attrib in BE ON WELFARE, to be in receipt of a benefit; PUT THE WELFARE ONTO A PERSON, to report someone to the Child Welfare Division as an (often alleged) inadequate parent (ca1958 R. Mason `a common expression'); a WELFARE KID, a child fostered by the State, or under State supervision cf W3 <i>welfare work</i>  
> **Grace** 'Got the Welfare on them once, but you see the girl's in good health, good enough clothes, everything. The Welfare talked to the school, found nothing wrong...' *(2001,Dogside Story,100)* [NOTES: = Child Welfare]

### welfare bludger
  
noun.  
> Kiwis have supported `cradle to the grave' state socialism, but rail against the unemployed and `welfare bludgers' ... *(Dec 27, 1987,Dominion Sunday Times,6)*

### welfare guardian
  
noun. a person appointed by a family court to make decisions for someone who lacks capacity to understand the nature or consequence of decisions relating to their personal welfare or cannot communicate them.  
[NOTES: Introduced by the Protection of Personal and Property Rights Act 1988.]

### welfare worker
  
noun.  
> **HYDE** I first heard of Stark when a very glum welfare worker ... informed me that he had declared ... he was prepared to steal them. *(1935,Passport to Hell,9)*

### well dogged up
  
phrase. [Rural] [mustering and shepherding] having an effective team of dogs.  
> **STACEY** I was with Alf Barnett and the rest of the gang who were well dogged up with three heading dogs. *(1996,Tales of a Tussock Jumper,82)*

### well I'll be photographed
  
exclamation. a euphemism for 'well I'll be fucked!'  
> **HENDERSON** `Well. I'll be photographed!' exclaimed the digger. *(1945,Gunner Inglorious,153)*

### well in
  
adjective. well informed, knowledgeable  
> A cow-cocky who was reckoned to be pretty well in said he'd killed and eaten a lot of meat in his time, and he knew for a fact that no man ... could eat a whole calf ... *(1977,Listener Short Stories,21)* [NOTES: 1945 Sargeson `Showers' in 1977 Listener Short Stories 21]

### well raced
  
adjective. [of dairy farms] with an effective system of lanes for moving cows to and from a cowshed.  
> Dairy Exporter 76 Flat to rolling. Well fenced and raced. *(1993)*  
> Daily News Jan 22. 7 Good flat 37 hectare dairy farm on target for 30,00kg/MS. Well raced and subdivided for easy management .. This is a well subdivided runoff, well raced, good stock yard and hay sheds, town water. *(2001)*  
> Daily News April 26. 16 The property is well raced but it's easy to get lost on because it's quite flat and nondescript. *(2001)*  
> Sunday Star Times Oct 28 C10 Large Northland dairy unit 234 ha well subdivided, very well fenced and raced. *(2001)*  
> Daily News Nov 1. 17 Large dairy farm. Very well raced. *(2001)*  
> Realeanz.co.nz Feb 19 Easy contour with hay country. Well raced with cattle underpass. *(2002)*

### well sprung
  
adjective. a. having plenty of money; well-heeled [nf P] ca1950 at least, Wellington; b. drunk  
> **ANTHONY** He never drank whiskey until he was `well sprung' *(1927,Follow the Call,18,1936)*  
> **ANTHONY** He never drank spirits until he was `well sprung', and as soon as he started on the whiskey that was the end of him. *(1924,Follow the Call,7,1975)*

### well wooled
  
adjective.  
> **RICHARDS** We will be on hoggets all day. They are big and well wooled, but will be good cutting. *(1986,Off the Sheep's Back,75)*

### well, what do you know
  
exclamation.  
> **BAKER** ...Well, what do you know!, another discrediting cant phrase... *(1941,NZ Slang,50)*  
> **PARTRIDGE** <i>Well, what do you know! </i>A c.p. expressive of incredulous surprise: New Zealand (and elsewhere) since ca. 1918. B., 1941, 1942. *(1949,Dictionary of Slang Addenda,1219)*  
> **McGILL** <i>well, what do you know?</i> mild, rhetorical, mock incredulity; eg Well, what do you know if it ain't old Sumpoil himself. Can't keep away from the trough, eh?' c. 1920, elsewhere later. *(1988,Dictionary of Kiwi Slang, A,121)*

### well-crimped
  
adjective. [of fleece wool] tightly corrugated.

### well-dogged
  
adjective. [of shepherds and musterers] having an effective team of dogs.

> DU FAUR Sheepfarming for Profit 20 It may be possible to purchase well-framed ewes in store condition ... [good-framed] *(1966)*

### well-gone
  
adjective. much in love

### well-gone
  
adjective.

> ... `blinded with science' comes from boxing, as does `well gone.' *(Apr 2, 1938,Press, The,18)* [NOTES: Mcnab NZ Slang]

### well-gone
  
adjective. very drunk

noun. a bright green gecko, often with white patches down its spine, capable of vocalising loudly when threatened.

> Species present are common skink, spotted skink,common gecko, forest gecko, and Wellington green gecko. *(Aug 5, 2009,Wairarapa News,2)*

### Wellington swell
  
noun.

> A WELLINGTON SWELL MOBSMAN CAUGHT. *(Dec 16, 1896,Bay of Plenty Times,5)*  
> That chic, petite joli chere [sic], Miss Olive Armstrong, who held her daily levee of Wellington swells and young bloods, at the Clarendon, left for Christchurch on a holiday trip on Monday. *(Aug 25, 1906,NZ Truth,1)*  
> Miss Anita Primrose, a quick-change artist, gave several clever characterisations, whick included "Dublin Bay," "A Wellington Swell," "The Ice Man," and a war monologue, "Antinio." *(Jan 25, 1916,Evening Post,3)*  
> **THOMPSON** ...it is worthy of notice, that the colonists, soon after the formation of all the settlements, acquired distinguishing epithets; thus there was an Auckland cove, a Wellington swell, a Nelson snob, a Taranaki exquisite, an Otago cockney, and a Canterbury pilgrim. These epithets, almost already forgotten, are too characteristic to be buried in oblivion, as each originated in some peculiarity of the leading colonists. The Otago settlers derived theirs from their ignorance of the country in which they lived; the Auckland people from their extensive Sydney connexions; and the origin of the other names are sufficiently obvious without explanation. *(1859,Story of New Zealand, The,2/188)* [NOTES: also in 1941 Baker, p. 24]

### Wellington Tenths
  
noun.  
See: tenth(s)  
> He was a founding member of the Wellington Tenths Trust. In fact, he was at a day-long meeting of the Tenths Trust the day before he died. *(Jun 1998,Cuisine,No. 22: 9)*  
> At the time this column was written, the Waitangi Tribunal was hearing a case brought before it by the Wellington Tenths Trust for a fair return on the leases of the "tenths"... One of the conditions of the purchase [by the NZ Company] was the setting aside of one-tenth of each block of land as a reserve for the tribes who were willing to sell to the company. *(Apr 22, 1991,Listener (New Zealand Listener),40)*  
> **SUTHERLAND** In addition to the trade goods, certain lands [in Wellington] known as `Native Tenths' were set aside as reserves for the natives who signed the deed. [fn] At Wanganui, Brooke, an interpreter, explained similar reservations by marking out a chequer board and informing the natives `that nine portions would be occupied by the Europeans, and the tenth would be for themselves.' *(1941,Numismatic History of NZ,40)*  
> [The retirement village]  will be built by Wellington Tenths Trust, which owns the land, and retirement operators, Summers Management Group... The committee said the proposed development represented a new era in the use of the Tenths Trust's tribal land. *(Sep 28, 2000,Evening Post,2)*  
> **COWAN** [Quoting the Land Claims Court, Wellington in 1842] Barrett was afterwards asked, `Did you tell the natives who signed the deed that one-tenth of the land described should be reserved for the use of themselves and their families, ... *(1922,NZ Wars, The,1/443)*  
> Athletic Park, and two other Wellington sites, is owned by the Wellington Tenths (the name refers to the one-tenth share allocated to the Maori owners by the New Zealand Company last century) who lease the ground to the Wellington Rugby Football Union. *(Jan 2, 1981,Evening Post,16)*  
> The North Island Tenths, also known as the Wellington Tenths because of the location of the land, are the beneficial owners of one-tenth of the land ceded by Maori chiefs and reserved from sale to the New Zealand Company in 1839. *(Apr 12, 1985,Evening Post,1)*

### Wellingtonian
  
noun.  
> Being civilised people, Wellingtonians use vinaigrette instead of mayonnaise. *(Feb 2, 2000,Evening Post,17)*  
> DEATHS OF TWO WELL KNOWN WELLINGTONIANS. *(Jun 17, 1893,Auckland  Star,5)*  
> Carol singing will be featured at the Town Hall on Monday evening next, when Wellingtonians will assemble to sing real old carols with a real message in their lilt and words. *(Dec 20, 1924,Evening Post,9)*  
> WELLINGTONIANS AT THE R.A.F. SCHOOL. *(Feb 19, 1930,Evening Post,7)*  
> **SENIOR** I ... was admitted into the party of four - two Wellingtonians. one Londoner, one Queenslander *(1880,Travel & Trout,209)*  
> ... the geography of some prominent Wellingtonians on board was rather at fault. *(1901,TrNZI,33/500)*

### Wellingtonisation
  
noun.  
> The party's overnight Wellingtonisation will at least regularise its biorythmns [sic], *(Feb 8, 1997,Listener (New Zealand Listener),16)*

### Wellingtonist
  
noun.  
> **THOMPSON** {Provincialists] were anxious to be Wellingtonists or Aucklanders, not New Zealanders, .... *(1859,Story of New Zealand, The,2/223)* [NOTES: (obs.)]

### Wellingtons
  
noun.  
> **FERGUSON** About two companies later reached the Wellington's firing line. *(1921,History of the Canterbury Regiment,64)*  
> **WAITE** The Wellingtons were on the left, the Aucklands in the centre, and the Canterburys on the right. *(1919,New Zealanders at Gallipoli,125)*  
> **PUGSLEY** "The Wellingtons seemed to rise up each time from nowhere and the Turks were hurled back." *(1984,Gallipoli: the New Zealand Story,302)* [NOTES: 1929 quote]  
> **PUGSLEY** As it became light, the Wellingtons could see.. the Straits of the Dardenelles. *(1984,Gallipoli: the New Zealand Story,290)*  
> The Wellingtons then went out in front oftheir trench and waited for the barrageto lift. *(Jun 27, 1917,Chronicles NZEF,II/21 : 223)*

### well-noised
  
adjective. [of huntaway sheepdogs] with an appropriate bark.  
> GORDON Three Sheep and a Dog 7 If the sheep were to be turned or slewed to the left, a well-noised huntaway, able to get good effect from a distance, would angle further to the right and literally blow the mob left. *(1998)*

### well-tussocked
  
adjective. [Rural] [of land] covered with tussock.  
> **GORDON** Open fronted, it [dog trial cookshop] looked out on to a big, well-tussocked long pull course. *(1998,Three Sheep and a Dog,218)*  
> **GORDON** Liberators got good views too, and those include a brief stint on top of Kyeburn's very tall and well-tussocked long pull course. *(1998,Three Sheep and a Dog,222)*

### well-woolled
  
adjective. [of sheep] with a generous, even, thick fleece.  
> in BUTLER First Year (1863) 124 .. this number of well-woolled sheep would be quite beyond one man's power. *(1860)*  
> GUTHRIE-SMITH Tutira 379 .. in a bad hogget season, the least well-woolled sheep survive. *(1921)*  
> NZ Farmer Jan 1. 52 Every care has been taken to breed strong constitutioned, good carcased, well-woolled sheep, true to type. *(1929)*  
> BOWEN Wool Away! 137 The Romney is a well-woolled sheep, covered right down on to the points. *(1955)*  
> STEVENS Sheep I 100 The top pens were a quality line of well-woolled ewes in good condition and attractively brought out. *(1958)*  
> MCLEOD Many a Glorious Morning 170 This year twelve thousand well-woolled Merinos had been shorn. *(1970)*  
> STACEY Tales of a Tussock Jumper 40 They were a good line of well-woolled forward stores. *(1996)*  
> Dominion Sept 27 C9 They are very good converters of average food , very fertile, quick maturing and are very well woolled with a quality fine wool. [well woolled; well-wooled] *(2002)*

### well-yarded
  
adjective. [of dog trial sheep] placed in a yard with ease.  
> GORDON Three Sheep & a Dog 127 Closing the gate on a well-yarded trio is very satisfying since it's the same as getting the sheep in on time for drafting or shearing. *(1998)*

### Welly-dweller
  
noun. a Wellingtonian  
> This is the side that encourage people to live here - the environment, the close-knit community atmosphere and comfortable lifestyle. It essentially gives us Welly-dwellers the best of both worlds - but city and small town. *(Oct 25, 2000,Capital Times,2)*

### Wellys
  
noun. [informal] Wellington  
[NOTES: hypocoristic form]  
> So when I'm not here in Wellys or on the road with the guys, you could find me back at home in Hawke's Bay eating paua and crayfish and right now whitebait. *(Oct 22, 2011,Your Weekend,14)*  
> A CENTRAL Wellington billboard saying "It took five losers to unseat Kerry - RIP democracy Wellys!" was swiftly dismantled at the behest of a "horrified" Kerry Prendergast. *(Oct 20, 2010,Dominion Post,B2)*

### Wellyville
  
noun. Wellington  
> Award-winning photographer comes to Wellyville. *(May 1, 2002,Capital Times,3)*  
> One of New Zealand's most successful contemporary rock bands, Wellyville's own Pacifier - the band formerly known as Shihad - spent years struggling in the underground rock secene before putting out a number of hit albums... *(Aug 7, 2002,Capital Times,13)*  
> In the latest Wellyville series to hit our television screens.. Scott Willis plays the worst kind of male slut - one on a quest.... Set in cafes and bars such as Co2, Zibibbo and StudioNine, it has a distinctive Wellyville flavour. *(Mar 27, 2002,Capital Times,12)*

### Welly-woman
  
noun. Wellington woman  
> Toogood gives an example of his devotion to the windy city by saying he's in a long distance relationship with a Welly-woman. *(Nov 2, 2000,Evening Post,17)*

### Wellywood
  
noun. Wellington  
[NOTES: named after The Lord of the Rings and other films had production offices in Wellington.]  
> [Hdg] Wellywood - can the boom be sustained? The Wellywood phenomenon that has Wellington buzzing got a fillip last week with projections showing film companies have spent as much as $450 million in the region in the year ended March 31. *(Dec 5, 2000,Evening Post,6)*  
> [Hdg] Welcome to Wellywood... Property prices in Seatoun are rising along with its reputation for being the Hollywood of the South Pacific. *(May 22, 1999,Dominion,19)*  
> Duncan Sarkies will remember 1999 as the year he made it in the moves. Not quite Hollywood, but Wellywood certainly. *(Jan 2000,North & South,24)*  
> It is particularly relevant to Wellington with its aspirations of becoming Wellywood. *(Apr 17, 2000,Dominion,10)*  
> [Hdg] Move over Hollywood - Wellywood is hitting the international stage... "The fact is the term Wellywood is still a 'tongue in cheek' journalist's invention and will remain so until we get serious about film in Wellington." *(Dec 15, 1999,Norwester,11)*  
> [Hdg] Wellywood spawns new TV series. *(Apr 20, 2000,Evening Post,1)*  
> Wellywood eyes Hong Kong cash. [hdg] Wellywood is angling to make more money and keep it through new co-production deals being sought with the Hong Kong film industry. *(Mar 26, 2009,Dominion Post,C3)*  
> The <i>Lord of the Rings</i> has gone but the action in Wellywood goes on. *(Mar 7, 2001,Capital Times)*  
> So that image of Wellington being the arts capital and Wellywood ...? *(Sep 2005,North & South,75)*  
> Already the <i>Dark Knight</i> series has inspired epic levels of boosterism in the capital's two newspapers, as reports trumpet all that good TV money coming into town, as helping to establish Wellington as something childishly referred to as Wellywood. *(Apr 8, 2000,Listener (New Zealand Listener),22)*  
> This is Wellywood and budding filmmakers have access to film production houses and polytechnics, edit-suites, some production houses have volunteered edit time and crews. *(Jun 8, 2000,City Voice,5)*  
> Wellington's "Wellywood" may never replace Hollywood as the centre of the world film industry but it has brought new opportunities for employment - and not just being an extra. *(Aug 9, 2000,Evening Post,27)*  
> We're so Wellywood. *(Nov 28, 2003,Radio NZ / National Radio)* [NOTES: Linda Clark, Nine to Noon]  
> NOT SO long ago, the rest of the country guffawed at Wellington planning to rename itself Wellywood. *(Oct 27, 2010,NZ Herald,A13)*

### Wellywooder
  
noun. a Wellingtonian  
See: Wellywood  
> A <i>Wellywooder</i> sitting beneath the kinetic sculpture was surprised to see that the tip of the orange wand is developing a lean to the south... *(Jan 25, 2006,Capital Times,31 , No 14, p.2)*

### WEMO
  
abbreviation. Wellington Emergency Management Office  
> The Wellington Emergency Management Office (WEMO) is eager to hear from good keen people who can assist in the city's community-based emergency centre... WEMO also wants to hear from anyone interested in training as a rural fire volunteer. *(Jun 2001,Absolutely Positively Wellington,2)*

### Wendy's
  
noun. ? a hamburger (?from Wendy's Hamburgers)  
> Take away a man's ability to provide the Wendy's and a video and they give up altogether - it's back to Friday night with the lads. *(Sep 16, 2001,Sunday Star Times,D2)*
